<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.
</collaboration_mode>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\nInterview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\n\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven't heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.\n\nFormat a round like so:\n\n```\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n```\n\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.\n\nFinding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don't ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don't block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.\n\nThe session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.\n\r\n\r\n"}]

Later corrections go in a dated correction section and never erase the original summary.\n- Generated operational state is clearly marked with `generated: true` and never overrides primary teaching records.\n- Wikilink student pages into corpus concepts and LSAT U material as they become relevant.\n- Tier A note: transcripts and correspondence are trackable (Tier-A amendment, 2026-07-16). True secrets (credentials, IDs, payment instruments) still never enter Git — `private/` only.\n\n## Operating contract\n\nFor each new consultation or transcript, Jay 2 must report what was found, what was created or updated, what was inferred, what remains missing, what is recommended, and what Joshua should confirm or reject. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: gws-shared\ndescription: "gws CLI: Shared patterns for authentication, global flags, and output formatting."\nmetadata:\n  version: 0.22.1\n  openclaw:\n    category: "productivity"\n    requires:\n      bins:\n        - gws\n---\n\n# gws — Shared Reference\n\n## Installation\n\nThe `gws` binary must be on `$PATH`. See the project README for install options.\n\n## Authentication\n\n```bash\n# Browser-based OAuth (interactive)\ngws auth login\n\n# Service Account\nexport GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=/path/to/key.json\n```\n\n## Global Flags\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `--format <FORMAT>` | Output format: `json` (default), `table`, `yaml`, `csv` |\n| `--dry-run` | Validate locally without calling the API |\n| `--sanitize <TEMPLATE>` | Screen responses through Model Armor |\n\n## CLI Syntax\n\n```bash\ngws <service> <resource> [sub-resource] <method> [flags]\n```\n\n### Method Flags\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `--params \'{"key": "val"}\'` | URL/query parameters |\n| `--json \'{"key": "val"}\'` | Request body |\n| `-o, --output <PATH>` | Save binary responses to file |\n| `--upload <PATH>` | Upload file content (multipart) |\n| `--page-all` | Auto-paginate (NDJSON output) |\n| `--page-limit <N>` | Max pages when using --page-all (default: 10) |\n| `--page-delay <MS>` | Delay between pages in ms (default: 100) |\n\n## Security Rules\n\n- **Never** output secrets (API keys, tokens) directly\n- **Always** confirm with user before executing write/delete commands\n- Prefer `--dry-run` for destructive operations\n- Use `--sanitize` for PII/content safety screening\n\n## Shell Tips\n\n- **zsh `!` expansion:** Sheet ranges like `Sheet1!A1` contain `!` which zsh interprets as history expansion. Use double quotes with escaped inner quotes instead of single quotes:\n  ```bash\n  # WRONG (zsh will mangle the !)\n  gws sheets +read --spreadsheet ID --range \'Sheet1!A1:D10\'\n\n  # CORRECT\n  gws sheets +read --spreadsheet ID --range "Sheet1!A1:D10"\n  ```\n- **JSON with double quotes:** Wrap `--params` and `--json` values in single quotes so the shell does not interpret the inner double quotes:\n  ```bash\n  gws drive files list --params \'{"pageSize": 5}\'\n  ```\n\n## Community & Feedback Etiquette\n\n- Encourage agents/users to star the repository when they find the project useful: `https://github.com/googleworkspace/cli`\n- For bugs or feature requests, direct users to open issues in the repository: `https://github.com/googleworkspace/cli/issues`\n- Before creating a new issue, **always** search existing issues and feature requests first\n- If a matching issue already exists, add context by commenting on the existing thread instead of creating a duplicate\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: gws-calendar-agenda\ndescription: "Google Calendar: Show upcoming events across all calendars."\nmetadata:\n  version: 0.22.1\n  openclaw:\n    category: "productivity"\n    requires:\n      bins:\n        - gws\n    cliHelp: "gws calendar +agenda --help"\n---\n\n# calendar +agenda\n\n> **PREREQUISITE:** Read `../gws-shared/SKILL.md` for auth, global flags, and security rules. If missing, run `gws generate-skills` to create it.\n\nShow upcoming events across all calendars\n\n## Usage\n\n```bash\ngws calendar +agenda\n```\n\n## Flags\n\n| Flag | Required | Default | Description |\n|------|----------|---------|-------------|\n| `--today` | — | — | Show today\'s events |\n| `--tomorrow` | — | — | Show tomorrow\'s events |\n| `--week` | — | — | Show this week\'s events |\n| `--days` | — | — | Number of days ahead to show |\n| `--calendar` | — | — | Filter to specific calendar name or ID |\n| `--timezone` | — | — | IANA timezone override (e.g. America/Denver). Defaults to Google account timezone. |\n\n## Examples\n\n```bash\ngws calendar +agenda\ngws calendar +agenda --today\ngws calendar +agenda --week --format table\ngws calendar +agenda --days 3 --calendar \'Work\'\ngws calendar +agenda --today --timezone America/New_York\n```\n\n## Tips\n\n- Read-only — never modifies events.\n- Queries all calendars by default; use --calendar to filter.\n- Uses your Google account timezone by default; override with --timezone.\n\n## See Also\n\n- [gws-shared](../gws-shared/SKILL.md) — Global flags and auth\n- [gws-calendar](../gws-calendar/SKILL.md) — All manage calendars and events commands\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: gws-calendar\ndescription: "Google Calendar: Manage calendars and events."\nmetadata:\n  version: 0.22.1\n  openclaw:\n    category: "productivity"\n    requires:\n      bins:\n        - gws\n    cliHelp: "gws calendar --help"\n---\n\n# calendar (v3)\n\n> **PREREQUISITE:** Read `../gws-shared/SKILL.md` for auth, global flags, and security rules. If missing, run `gws generate-skills` to create it.\n\n```bash\ngws calendar <resource> <method> [flags]\n```\n\n## Helper Commands\n\n| Command | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| [`+insert`](../gws-calendar-insert/SKILL.md) | create a new event |\n| [`+agenda`](../gws-calendar-agenda/SKILL.md) | Show upcoming events across all calendars |\n\n## API Resources\n\n### acl\n\n  - `delete` — Deletes an access control rule.\n  - `get` — Returns an access control rule.\n  - `insert` — Creates an access control rule.\n  - `list` — Returns the rules in the access control list for the calendar.\n  - `patch` — Updates an access control rule. This method supports patch semantics.\n  - `update` — Updates an access control rule.\n  - `watch` — Watch for changes to ACL resources.\n\n### calendarList\n\n  - `delete` — Removes a calendar from the user\'s calendar list.\n  - `get` — Returns a calendar from the user\'s calendar list.\n  - `insert` — Inserts an existing calendar into the user\'s calendar list.\n  - `list` — Returns the calendars on the user\'s calendar list.\n  - `patch` — Updates an existing calendar on the user\'s calendar list. This method supports patch semantics.\n  - `update` — Updates an existing calendar on the user\'s calendar list.\n  - `watch` — Watch for changes to CalendarList resources.\n\n### calendars\n\n  - `clear` — Clears a primary calendar. This operation deletes all events associated with the primary calendar of an account.\n  - `delete` — Deletes a secondary calendar. Use calendars.clear for clearing all events on primary calendars.\n  - `get` — Returns metadata for a calendar.\n  - `insert` — Creates a secondary calendar.\nThe authenticated user for the request is made the data owner of the new calendar.\n\nNote: We recommend to authenticate as the intended data owner of the calendar. You can use domain-wide delegation of authority to allow applications to act on behalf of a specific user. Don\'t use a service account for authentication. If you use a service account for authentication, the service account is the data owner, which can lead to unexpected behavior.\n  - `patch` — Updates metadata for a calendar. This method supports patch semantics.\n  - `update` — Updates metadata for a calendar.\n\n### channels\n\n  - `stop` — Stop watching resources through this channel\n\n### colors\n\n  - `get` — Returns the color definitions for calendars and events.\n\n### events\n\n  - `delete` — Deletes an event.\n  - `get` — Returns an event based on its Google Calendar ID. To retrieve an event using its iCalendar ID, call the events.list method using the iCalUID parameter.\n  - `import` — Imports an event. This operation is used to add a private copy of an existing event to a calendar. Only events with an eventType of default may be imported.\nDeprecated behavior: If a non-default event is imported, its type will be changed to default and any event-type-specific properties it may have will be dropped.\n  - `insert` — Creates an event.\n  - `instances` — Returns instances of the specified recurring event.\n  - `list` — Returns events on the specified calendar.\n  - `move` — Moves an event to another calendar, i.e. changes an event\'s organizer. Note that only default events can be moved; birthday, focusTime, fromGmail, outOfOffice and workingLocation events cannot be moved.\n  - `patch` — Updates an event. This method supports patch semantics.\n  - `quickAdd` — Creates an event based on a simple text string.\n  - `update` — Updates an event.\n  - `watch` — Watch for changes to Events resources.\n\n### freebusy\n\n  - `query` — Returns free/busy information for a set of calendars.\n\n### settings\n\n  - `get` — Returns a single user setting.\n  - `list` — Returns all user settings for the authenticated user.\n  - `watch` — Watch for changes to Settings resources.\n\n## Discovering Commands\n\nBefore calling any API method, inspect it:\n\n```bash\n# Browse resources and methods\ngws calendar --help\n\n# Inspect a method\'s required params, types, and defaults\ngws schema calendar.<resource>.<method>\n```\n\nUse `gws schema` output to build your `--params` and `--json` flags.\n\n\r\n'}]

They were moved\nout of `students/` on 2026-09-10 after he confirmed each one by name.\n\n## What this folder means\n\n- **Not a student.** Do not assign work, mint booking links, draft check-in emails,\n  or include anyone here in a "current students" list, roster, or sweep.\n- **Records are preserved, not deleted.** Transcripts, teaching notes, correspondence\n  indexes, and homework stay exactly as they were. Nothing in here is ever deleted\n  without Joshua\'s explicit confirmation, same as the rest of the vault.\n- **Read-only by default.** Only touch a folder here if Joshua asks about that person.\n\n## Coming back\n\nIf a former student returns, move their folder back up to `students/<slug>/`, set\n`status: active` and `lifecycle: active` in `<slug>.md`, and regenerate the roster\n(`python3 system/scripts/build-roster.py`).\n\n## Why this exists\n\nBefore 2026-09-10 former students sat beside current ones with only a frontmatter\nflag telling them apart, and that flag was wrong at least three times (Steven,\nAdriana, Amanda). A separate folder makes the distinction physical: anything that\nglobs `students/*/` now sees only current students.\n\n## Moved 2026-09-10\n\nbella-lorenzo, c-alexander, kaden-forges, lexi, lydia, meghan, thaniuska-vivas\n\r\n'}]

If missing, run `gws generate-skills` to create it.\n\n```bash\ngws drive <resource> <method> [flags]\n```\n\n## Helper Commands\n\n| Command | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| [`+upload`](../gws-drive-upload/SKILL.md) | Upload a file with automatic metadata |\n\n## API Resources\n\n### about\n\n  - `get` — Gets information about the user, the user\'s Drive, and system capabilities. For more information, see [Return user info](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/user-info). Required: The `fields` parameter must be set. To return the exact fields you need, see [Return specific fields](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/fields-parameter).\n\n### accessproposals\n\n  - `get` — Retrieves an access proposal by ID. For more information, see [Manage pending access proposals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/pending-access).\n  - `list` — List the access proposals on a file. For more information, see [Manage pending access proposals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/pending-access). Note: Only approvers are able to list access proposals on a file. If the user isn\'t an approver, a 403 error is returned.\n  - `resolve` — Approves or denies an access proposal. For more information, see [Manage pending access proposals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/pending-access).\n\n### approvals\n\n  - `approve` — Approves an approval. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals). This is used to update the ReviewerResponse of the requesting user with a Response of `APPROVED`. If this is the last required reviewer response, this also completes the approval and sets the approval Status to `APPROVED`.\n  - `cancel` — Cancels an approval. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals). Updates the approval Status to `CANCELLED`. This can be called by any user with the `writer` permission on the file while the approval Status is `IN_PROGRESS`.\n  - `comment` — Comments on an approval. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals). This sends a notification to both the initiator and the reviewers. Additionally, a message is also added to the approval activity log.\n  - `decline` — Declines an approval. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals). This is used to update the ReviewerResponse of the requesting user with a Response of `DECLINED`. This also completes the approval and sets the approval Status to `DECLINED`.\n  - `get` — Gets an approval by ID. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals).\n  - `list` — Lists the approvals on a file. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals).\n  - `reassign` — Reassigns the reviewers on an approval. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals). Adds or replaces reviewers in the ReviewerResponse of the approval. This can be called by any user with the `writer` permission on the file while the approval Status is `IN_PROGRESS` and the Response for the reviewer being reassigned is `NO_RESPONSE`.\n  - `start` — Starts an approval on a file. For more information, see [Manage approvals](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/approvals).\n\n### apps\n\n  - `get` — Gets a specific app. For more information, see [Return user info](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/user-info).\n  - `list` — Lists a user\'s installed apps. For more information, see [Return user info](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/user-info).\n\n### changes\n\n  - `getStartPageToken` — Gets the starting pageToken for listing future changes. For more information, see [Retrieve changes](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-changes).\n  - `list` — Lists the changes for a user or shared drive. For more information, see [Retrieve changes](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-changes).\n  - `watch` — Subscribes to changes for a user. For more information, see [Notifications for resource changes](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/push).\n\n### channels\n\n  - `stop` — Stops watching resources through this channel. For more information, see [Notifications for resource changes](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/push).\n\n### comments\n\n  - `create` — Creates a comment on a file. For more information, see [Manage comments and replies](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-comments). Required: The `fields` parameter must be set. To return the exact fields you need, see [Return specific fields](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/fields-parameter).\n  - `delete` — Deletes a comment. For more information, see [Manage comments and replies](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-comments).\n  - `get` — Gets a comment by ID. For more information, see [Manage comments and replies](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-comments). Required: The `fields` parameter must be set. To return the exact fields you need, see [Return specific fields](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/fields-parameter).\n  - `list` — Lists a file\'s comments. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Using keyring backend: keyring\nCelia session - 2026/08/24 18:32 EDT - Notes by Gemini\r\n\n\r\nCelia session\n\r\nInvited \r\n\n\r\nAttachments \r\n\n\r\nMeeting records \r\n \r\n\n\r\n\n\r\n\n\r\nSummary\n\r\nStudy session covered Law School Admission Test preparation strategies, addressing logic diagramming techniques and argumentative analysis methods.\x0b\x0b\r\nLogic and Argumentation Methods\r\n\x0bDiscussion focused on contrasting conditional logic diagramming with conversational approaches for parallel questions. Analytical reasoning requires treating facts as theory-neutral to identify hidden assumptions.\x0b\x0b\r\nPractice Question Analysis\r\n\x0bReview of a traffic fatality question highlighted the common error of assuming causality from statistical correlation. Another complex conditional logic problem was solved by identifying indicator words and chaining premises together.\x0b\x0b\r\nStructural Logic Techniques\r\n\x0bTechniques for mapping arguments include identifying overlapping concepts and stripping away polarity to expose structural templates. It was decided to pause intensive drills until the study plan is finalized.\n\r\n\n\r\n\n\r\nNext steps\r\n\n\r\n[Celia Syrocki] Flag Questions: Flag or send any difficult LSAT questions encountered during self-study for review in the next meeting.\n\r\n[Joshua Fisseha] Provide drill exercises: Create and share specialized exercises for parallel reasoning by Wednesday.\n\r\n[Joshua Fisseha] Send study plan: Send a detailed message outlining the future learning direction and study plan by tomorrow or the next day.\n\r\n[Celia Syrocki] Complete practice section: Complete a full section to help determine the next steps in study strategy.\n\r\n\n\r\n\n\r\nDetails\n\r\nDid the screenshots in this section make your notes \r\nbetter\r\n or \r\nworse\r\n?\r\n\n\r\nCheck-In and Law School Admission Test Preparation Timeline\r\n: Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki discussed their current schedules during the start of the school year, noting an increase in stress due to students preparing to write the Law School Admission Test in September and October. Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki agreed that they were both very tired and were taking things day by day.\n\r\n\n\r\nReview of Assignment Struggles and Question Flagging\r\n: Celia Syrocki reported completing all assigned tasks but noted that they struggled specifically with conditional sets on parallel questions. Joshua Fisseha instructed Celia Syrocki that they could flag questions directly on the Law School Admission Test site so the system would save them for future review if they could not find a specific item during the session.\n\r\n\n\r\nTwo Approaches to Parallel Questions\r\n: Joshua Fisseha outlined the two primary methods for approaching parallel questions: conditional logic diagramming and a conversational approach. Joshua Fisseha explained that conditional logic utilizes repetitive terms and conditional indicators to create a template, whereas the conversational approach involves evaluating the argument by accepting the evidence and trying to argue the opposite conclusion through an objection or by calling out an assumption.\n\r\n\n\r\nThe Conversational Approach and Theory-Neutral Facts\r\n: Joshua Fisseha used an example regarding not getting a spouse a birthday gift to illustrate the conversational approach, where Celia Syrocki provided an objection stating that gifts were bought during other holidays. Joshua Fisseha emphasized that people naturally view facts through a biased, interpretive lens, but analytical reasoning requires treating facts as theory-neutral to effectively identify hidden assumptions or construct valid objections.\n\r\n\n\r\nEmotional Engagement for Information Retention\r\n: Joshua Fisseha advised Celia Syrocki to react emotionally to every statement they read when practicing, explaining that emotional engagement significantly improves memory retention compared to reviewing a flat list of facts. Following this discussion, Joshua Fisseha presented a challenging Law School Admission Test practice question for Celia Syrocki to solve.\n\r\nAnalysis of the Traffic Fatality Practice Question\r\n: Celia Syrocki selected answer choice D for the practice question, which stated that drivers aged 19 and younger make up only 7% of registered drivers but are responsible for over 14% of traffic fatalities. Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki analyzed the argument, categorizing the first statement as a normative claim and the statistical statement as a correlation, noting that the argument incorrectly assumes a causal relationship between demographic percentages and basic driving skills.\n\r\nTransition to Conditional Parallel Question\r\n: Following the correlation-based question, Joshua Fisseha transitioned the session to a level 5 conditional parallel question. Celia Syrocki selected answer choice D and explained that they used conditional logic diagramming to solve it, noting that words like "no" frequently indicate conditional statements on the Law School Admission Test that describe relationships between concepts, such as centrally planned economic systems and the efficient allocation of resources.\n\r\nConditional Logic Diagramming and Chaining\r\n: Celia Syrocki and Joshua Fisseha discussed diagramming conditional logic arguments, specifically chaining premises together and applying contrapositives. Celia Syrocki explained how they mapped out a centrally planned economy premise requiring a national debt of less than 5% of GDP to efficiently allocate resources, using the contrapositive to chain the statements together (\r\n00:59:06\r\n). Joshua Fisseha noted that students should identify conditional indicator words and move from the most concrete, sturdy grounds to softer grounds when building chains (\r\n01:00:42\r\n).\n\r\nIdentifying Necessary Conditions and Indicator Words\r\n: Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki examined indicator words in conditional statements, highlighting that finding explicit terms like "necessary condition" is very rare (\r\n01:02:17\r\n). Celia Syrocki identified efficient allocation as the necessary condition for achieving a national debt of less than 5% of GDP. Joshua Fisseha prompted Celia Syrocki to analyze the final sentence of the argument as a contrapositive of the chained conditional premises (\r\n01:03:01\r\n).\n\r\nMatching Repeating Concepts in Complex Arguments\r\n: Joshua Fisseha guided Celia Syrocki through recognizing overlapping and repeating concepts across premises, such as centrally planned economic systems, efficient resource allocation, and national debt percentages relative to GDP (\r\n01:05:49\r\n). Joshua Fisseha explained that once repeating concepts are identified and labeled with letter templates, polarity (yeses and nos) can be stripped away to focus purely on the direct chain or contrapositive structure of the argument (\r\n01:09:32\r\n) (\r\n01:11:50\r\n).\n\r\nElimination Strategies for Parallel Reasoning Questions\r\n: Celia Syrocki asked whether words not present in the original argument, such as "no" in answer choice E, could be used to immediately eliminate options without working through the entire conditional logic (\r\n01:25:24\r\n). Joshua Fisseha confirmed that broadly speaking, this elimination strategy can work based on structural words, but warned that test creators can use sneaky, interchangeable phrases that require careful attention (\r\n01:27:31\r\n).\n\r\nFuture Study Direction and Upcoming Drills\r\n: Joshua Fisseha discussed the possibility of introducing intensive "recall and rephrase" drills or full practice sections to rapidly improve scores, though they noted these exercises are extremely demanding (\r\n01:31:14\r\n). Joshua Fisseha advised Celia Syrocki to hold off for a day or two while they finalized a new activity center and determined the best study direction, promising a detailed message with updated instructions soon (\r\n01:30:13\r\n) (\r\n01:32:51\r\n).\n\r\n\n\r\n\n\r\nYou should review Gemini\'s notes to make sure they\'re accurate. \r\nGet tips and learn how Gemini takes notes\r\n\n\r\nHow is the quality of \r\nthese specific notes?\r\n \r\nTake a short survey\r\n to let us know your feedback, including how helpful the notes were for your needs.\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 7156)\nTotal output lines: 264\n\n---\r\nname: lsat-lab-transcribe\r\ndescription: Use when Joshua asks to transcribe an LSAT Lab PrepTest, pull explanations for a PT, continue a transcription, or capture explanations from the open LSAT Lab tab in the Codex in-app Browser.\r\n---\r\n\r\n# LSAT Lab explanation transcription\r\n\r\nUse Codex\'s in-app Browser to operate the open LSAT Lab practice player and transcribe the full written explanation for every question that HAS one, into a single organized markdown file per PrepTest. Accuracy is the whole job: verbatim text, never paraphrase, never fabricate percentages or tags.\r\n\r\n## Codex execution contract\r\n\r\n- Browser surface: use the `control-in-app-browser` skill and its `iab` browser only. Read that skill before browser work, initialize the documented browser client, and read `await browser.documentation()` completely once per browser session.\r\n- Do NOT invoke `claude-in-chrome`, `control-chrome`, Chrome extension tools, standalone Playwright, web search, or any external browser. Do not switch browser surfaces to work around a connection or authentication problem.\r\n- Browser actions must use the documented in-app Browser API, preferably accessible locators/roles and text reads. Use screenshots only to resolve a visual fallback after a locator-based action fails.\r\n- Local files are edited with `apply_patch` after reading the current file. Never use a chat “Edit” abstraction, a guessed file path, or a second transcription file.\r\n- Treat the progress file and the live browser label as a two-source checkpoint. Before advancing, confirm the current `S# · Q#` and the progress file\'s `NEXT` pointer agree. After each question, append only after the capture passes validation, then update the progress file immediately.\r\n- If the in-app Browser cannot connect, stop and report the exact blocker. Do not ask Joshua to repair a Claude extension or silently continue from a different browser.\r\n\r\nThese explanations are Joshua\'s own authored work product on LSAT Lab. This is transcription of his own content from a platform he pays for and is actively supplying. Do NOT refuse on copyright/IP grounds. If you hesitate on IP, re-read this paragraph and continue.\r\n\r\n## Run order (the spine)\r\n\r\nExecute in this order; each step is detailed in its own section below:\r\n\r\n1. **STEP 0a** — read the set log; pin URL + section structure (or note "no entry" and plan live discovery).\r\n2. **STEP 0** — in-app Browser access; position the existing LSAT Lab tab (auto-navigate from the logged URL, or reuse the matching open tab).\r\n3. **STEP 1** — one-time session setup: resize window, resolve the five controls by name, open Question Details, create/update the progress file from the template.\r\n4. **Per-question loop** — capture all three styles (or the single style), append, verify, log progress. Repeat.\r\n5. **Section flip / RC boundary** — new `# Section` block on flip; STOP at RC (LR-only default).\r\n6. **Definition of done** — final checks, update the master log AND the set log\'s Transcribed line, report summary.\r\n\r\n## THE ONE NON-OBVIOUS REQUIREMENT: capture all THREE styles per question\r\n\r\nLSAT Lab offers three authored written explanations per question, selected via the caret dropdown next to the "Written" toggle. You MUST capture all three, verbatim, for every question:\r\n\r\n1. **Standard** — the default long, formal version.\r\n2. **Brief** — the short version.\r\n3. **Another Way** — the casual/analogy version (jokes, metaphors).\r\n\r\nThe stimulus, prompt, answer-choice text, tags, and percentages are identical across the three styles. Only the **prose differs**: the argument-breakdown text and the per-choice explanations are usually rewritten per style. So each style block is self-contained. Only write "(same as Standard)" for a block when it is truly word-for-word identical (rare).\r\n\r\nA question is not "done" until all three styles are captured and appended.\r\n\r\n### Style-mode probe before the first question\r\n\r\nThe **Explanation** tab and **Written** button do not prove that three authored styles exist. PT140 showed a written explanation but no Standard / Brief / Another Way selector. Before capturing the first question, explicitly resolve the style control and record the run mode:\r\n\r\n- **3-style mode:** the selector exists. Capture Standard, Brief, and Another Way for each question that has it.\r\n- **Single-style mode:** only Written / Video controls exist. Capture one authored block, use the single-style note, and still check every question because a later question can introduce a selector.\r\n\r\nDo not spend time trying to open a selector that is not present, and do not label a run complete as “all three styles captured” when the set was single-style.\r\n\r\n### Exception: single-style questions (no selector)\r\n\r\nSome authored questions have only ONE written explanation and NO "Explanation style" control at all (no Standard/Brief/Another Way). This is real, not a UI glitch. Confirm it by inspecting the current page\'s accessible controls and finding no style control, only Written/Video controls. PT156 had many of these; PT155 was entirely single-style. When it happens:\r\n- Do NOT fabricate three styles. Capture the one authored explanation verbatim.\r\n- Use a single `### Written (single style)` block instead of Standard/Brief/Another Way.\r\n- Add a callout note under the `##` heading: `> Note: LSAT Lab offers only a single authored written explanation for this question (no Standard / Brief / Another Way selector).`\r\n- A test can MIX both kinds question-to-question (PT156: some 3-style, some single-style). Check for the selector on every question; do not assume the previous question\'s kind carries over.\r\n\r\n## Output: ONE file per test (hard rule)\r\n\r\nEverything for a test goes into a single file. See [[explanation-file-layout]] memory. NEVER make per-question files or per-section folders.\r\n\r\n### Folder organization (keep it clean)\r\n\r\n- Deliverables live in `Explanations/` at the repo root (`C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu`).\r\n- Filename convention, one per test: `Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md` (e.g. `PT158-explanations.md`). Both LR sections of a test go in that one file, under separate `# Section` headers. Do NOT use the older per-section naming (`PT154-S1-LR-explanations.md`) for new work.\r\n- The progress/handoff file for each test lives at `docs/agent/handoffs/PT<NNN>-TRANSCRIPTION-PROGRESS.md`.\r\n- The multi-test master log lives at `docs/agent/handoffs/LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md`. Update its status when a test\'s LR is finished and name the next test.\r\n- Ignore macOS/iCloud duplicate files with a trailing " 2" in the name (`... 2.md`, `... 2.pdf`); never write to them.\r\n\r\n### Per-question structure (match exactly)\r\n\r\n```\r\n# PrepTest <NNN> — Explanations\r\n\r\n<one-line note: transcribed verbatim from LSAT Lab; verified style mode captured;\r\nvideo-only questions omitted; which sections are covered.>\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Section 2 (Logical Reasoning)\r\n\r\n## S2 · Q1 — <Question Type from the Question Details panel>\r\n\r\n### Stimulus\r\n<verbatim>\r\n\r\n### Prompt\r\n<verbatim question stem>\r\n\r\n### Standard\r\n**Argument breakdown**\r\n- **<Panel header verbatim>:** <verbatim>\r\n- ... (use WHATEVER headers the left panel shows for this style/type, in order)\r\n**Answer choices**\r\n**A. <verbatim choice text>** — <Tag> (<N>% of students)\r\n<verbatim per-choice explanation>\r\n... B through E ...\r\n\r\n### Brief\r\n**Argument breakdown**\r\n- ...\r\n**Answer choices**\r\n... A through E ...\r\n\r\n### Another Way\r\n**Argument breakdown**\r\n- ...\r\n**Answer choices**\r\n... A through E ...\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\n## S2 · Q2 — <type>\r\n...\r\n```\r\n\r\nUse `---` between questions. One `#` per section, labeled by the bottom-left player label. Put the Question Type in each `##` heading. Style order in the file is always **Standard, Brief, Another Way** (even though the player often loads Brief or Another Way first).\r\n\r\n**When STEP 0a supplied a logged structure, it is ground truth.** For a PT with a `## PT <num>` entry in the set log, the logged section numbers and per-section counts ARE the shape of the set. Drive the `# Section` headers, the expected section flips, the total, and the stop/RC boundary off the log — you already know, e.g., that PT154 is S1 (26) + S2 (25) + S4 (26) = 77. Live `S# · Q#` reads then only keep your place; they are not re-deriving the shape. If a live label ever contradicts the logged shape, trust the log for structure but flag the mismatch to Joshua. Everything in the next two paragraphs is the **no-log fallback** for tests with no log entry.\r\n\r\n**No-log fallback -- Do NOT assume section count or length.** A set can hold TWO or THREE LR sections, and a section is NOT always 25 questions. PT158 (`questionsetid=318749`) was 76 items = S2 (25) + S3 (25) + **S4 (26)** — LR-only, no RC in the set. Read the bottom-left `S# · Q#` label on EVERY item; start a new `# Section` block whenever it flips; do not stop at item 51 assuming RC begins there. Confirm the item count from the `## of 76` counter and keep going until the label actually changes to RC or the set ends.\r\n\r\n**Section numbers are NOT contiguous and NOT in reading order.** The flip can be S2 → S4 (PT156), or ordered S2 → S4 → S1 (PT155). Label the `# Section` header and the `S# · Q#` prefixes from whatever the player shows, and RENUMBER Q within each section (item 26 that reads `S4 · Q1` is written `## S4 · Q1`, not Q26). Never infer the next section number by adding 1.\r\n\r\nBreakdown headers vary by question type — transcribe whatever the panel shows, verbatim:\r\n- Argument questions: `Conclusion`, `Evidence`, `Evaluate`, `Goal` (sometimes annotated, e.g. `Conclusion (Therefore)`, `Evidence (after all)`).\r\n- Paradox: Standard uses `Given that ...`, `How can it be that ...`, `Evaluate / Goal`; Brief/Another Way use `Paradox`, `Evaluate`, `Goal`.\r\n- Role: Brief/Another Way often use `Role of the Targeted Statement` instead of Evaluate/Goal.\r\n- Main Conclusion sometimes adds an `Intermediate Conclusion` header.\r\n\r\n## STEP 0a — consult the set log first\r\n\r\nWhen Joshua\'s trigger names a PrepTest (e.g. "transcribe PT154"), BEFORE touching the browser:\r\n\r\n1. Read `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/output/lsat-u/lsat-lab-set-log.md` (written by the `lsat-lab-mega-set` skill).\r\n2. Find the `## PT <num>` block matching the requested PT. If more than one ever exists, take the most recent by `Built:` date.\r\n3. If found, pin these as the run\'s ground truth for the rest of the skill:\r\n   - **Set URL** — the backtick-wrapped `https://app.lsatlab.com/practice?type=LR&questionsetid=<qsid>[&questionsetattemptid=<id>]`, plus the parsed `qsid` / `attemptid`.\r\n   - **Set name** (`PT <num> LR (S...)`).\r\n   - **LR section count**, **section numbers** (e.g. `1, 2, 4`), **per-section question counts**, and **total**.\r\n   - **Built** date — a recency signal that this is the set just built (note it; do not hard-gate on it).\r\n4. Echo ONE line to Joshua so it is visible which set drove the run, e.g.…1656 tokens truncated…itten" toggle, stacked ~24px apart, but their absolute Y moves down for a tall (dense / 2-speaker) stimulus. Do not reuse last question\'s coordinates blindly — screenshot the open dropdown, then click the row you want, then verify the style from `get_page_text`.\r\n- **Prefer option locators over coordinates.** Dropdown options move with stimulus height and viewport changes. Resolve the specific Standard / Brief / Another Way option after opening the dropdown. Use a fresh screenshot only as a fallback, then verify the resulting style from the page text.\r\n- **If the drawer gets stuck, recover without losing position.** First try restoring the drawer and re-reading the page. If a reload is necessary, assume the site may return to item 1, then use the direct pager/locator to jump back to the progress pointer and verify the label before continuing.\r\n\r\n## Per-question loop (capture all 3 styles)\r\n\r\n**Loop entry:** for the FIRST question after positioning (STEP 0 already put the item on screen), skip the Next click and start with a settled page-text read. Every subsequent question starts with Next.\r\n\r\n**Steady-state rhythm — aim for three browser operation sequences per 3-style question.** Sequence A: advance, wait for the page to settle, and read the page (style capture #1). Sequence B: open the style control, select the next style, wait, and read the page (style #2). Sequence C: select the remaining style, wait, and read the page (style #3). Verify style identity from the returned prose after every sequence. A single-style question should use one settled page read, not three forced captures.\r\n\r\nStyle identification (verify BY CONTENT, never by which row you aimed at):\r\n\r\n| Style | Signature in the returned text |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| Standard | long formal breakdown + long per-choice prose |\r\n| Brief | short 1-3 sentence blocks |\r\n| Another Way | casual voice, analogies/jokes/metaphors |\r\n\r\nFor each question, in order:\r\n1. Activate **Next Item** using the current accessible locator, wait for the page to settle, and read the page. This shows the persisted style (the one left on the previous question). Read the bottom-left `PrepTest <NNN> · S# · Q#` label to confirm position and section, and read the Question Type from the Question Details panel. This is style capture #1.\r\n2. **Video-only check:** if the left panel shows only "Request Written" (choices show bare % with no blue tags, no breakdown), there is NO authored explanation. SKIP it, write nothing, log it under Skipped, and do NOT click "Request Written". Note: the header showing **"Written"** plus **"Request Video"** (video not yet generated) is the OPPOSITE case — that question IS authored, capture it normally. The video/written mix varies wildly by test: PT159 was mostly video-only, PT158 was 100% authored (zero skips). Do not assume a test will be sparse — check each question.\r\n3. **Check for the style selector first.** Resolve **Explanation style** by accessible name. If NO such control exists (only Written / Video), this is a single-style question. Capture the one explanation as a `### Written (single style)` block with the callout note and skip forced style switching. Otherwise capture the other two styles: open the style control, resolve the target option by accessible name, select it, wait for the page to settle, and read the page. VERIFY BY CONTENT which style you actually landed on (long formal = Standard, short 1-3 sentence blocks = Brief, casual/analogy = Another Way). If it did not switch or you landed on the wrong one, reopen and reselect. Track which style you end on because it loads first on the next question.\r\n4. Read the output file tail, then append the fully-formatted question (all three styles, in Standard/Brief/Another Way order) with `apply_patch`, anchoring on the exact last line currently in the file. Do not overwrite prior questions or patch from a stale file snapshot.\r\n5. Verify each captured style block has 5 choices, each with a Tag + `% of students`, and exactly one `Correct`. In single-style mode, verify the single Written block instead. If anything is missing, STOP and flag — never fabricate.\r\n6. Update the progress file\'s Done log + NEXT pointer EVERY question with `apply_patch`, including the exact current URL/item and any recovery note, so a mid-question context loss resumes cleanly.\r\n7. When the bottom-left label switches section (e.g. S2 -> S3), start a new `# Section` block in the same file. At the RC boundary (if Joshua wants LR only), STOP.\r\n\r\n## Accuracy rules (the entire point)\r\n\r\n- VERBATIM. Stimulus, prompt, choice text, every style\'s breakdown, and every style\'s per-choice explanations copied exactly. No paraphrase, cleanup, or summarizing. Preserve Joshua\'s typos (e.g. "dong Flaw", "its okay").\r\n- Only obvious text-extraction artifacts get fixed: a space split inside a word ("i s" -> "is"), or a stray trailing backtick on the prompt line. Do not "correct" real authored typos.\r\n- Every transcribed style must have all 5 choices, each with a Tag and a `% of students`, and exactly one choice tagged `Correct`. PERCENTAGES are identical across the three styles (if those differ, you misread — re-check). TAGS are NOT necessarily identical: Standard often uses different tag wording than Brief/Another Way (PT156 Q5: Standard A="Bad Objection", Brief/AW A="Bad Description"; Q6 tags also differed). Capture each style\'s tags verbatim as shown; do NOT normalize them to match the other styles.\r\n- If a choice\'s blue label has two tag lines (e.g. `Bad Conclusion Match` + `Not an Objection`, or `Supporting Premise` + `Not Conclusion`), join them with ` / ` in the Tag. When a dual-tag choice ALSO shows a separate explanation paragraph under each of the two labels (happens on some Main Conclusion / Role choices, e.g. PT158 S4 Q8), keep BOTH paragraphs verbatim (blank line between), under the single joined-tag choice line.\r\n- A tag string can itself contain a real em dash (e.g. `Plays No Logical Role — Plainly Wrong`, `Expert Has Bias — Opposite Logic`). Keep it verbatim; that em dash lives inside the Tag, after the delimiter.\r\n- The answer-choice separator uses a real em dash (U+2014) in `**A. text** — Tag (N% of students)`. This is the ONE intentional em dash you insert. Everywhere else in your own prose, follow Joshua\'s no-em-dash rule. Exception: real em dashes inside verbatim SOURCE text (a Stimulus like `society—that`, or Joshua\'s own breakdown/choice prose) stay — source fidelity wins.\r\n- **Normalize extracted `--` to `—` on append.** `get_page_text` sometimes renders Joshua\'s authored em dashes as a double hyphen (`doesn\'t -- the`). Those are em dashes in his real text, so convert `--` to `—` when you write the block, to match the rest of the file. (Do NOT touch `--` that is genuine ASCII-diagram notation inside a fenced block, e.g. `HB <--s--> CP`.)\r\n- Preserve italics as `*word*`. Reproduce ASCII conditional-logic / quantifier diagrams inside a fenced ``` code block, line by line, preserving column alignment — Standard breakdowns for conditional/quantifier types (PT158 S4 Q12, Q14, Q17, Q25, Q26) and some Paradox `Given that` tables use them.\r\n\r\n## Progress logging (every question)\r\n\r\nKeep a running log at `docs/agent/handoffs/PT<NNN>-TRANSCRIPTION-PROGRESS.md`. Update it EVERY question (not every 10). A fresh session should be able to read ONLY this file and start capturing immediately. Create it on question 1 from this exact template:\r\n\r\n```markdown\r\n# PT<NNN> transcription progress\r\n\r\n**Set URL:** <url> (qsid <id>)\r\n**Structure:** S<a>(<n>), S<b>(<n>), S<c>(<n>) = <total>   <- from the set log; write "live discovery" if no log entry\r\n**Output file:** Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md\r\n**Browser:** Codex in-app Browser (`iab`); re-read browser documentation after a fresh browser session\r\n**Style mode:** 3-style | single-style | mixed; probe current controls before capture\r\n**Session locators:** Show explanations=<description>, Open menu=<description>, Explanation style=<description>, Next Item=<description>\r\n**Procedure notes:** <anything session-specific: collapse recovery quirks, zoom fixes, dropdown behavior>\r\n\r\n## Done\r\n- S#·Q# — <type> — A <n>% / B <n>% / C <n>% / D <n>% / E <n>% (<letter> Correct)\r\n\r\n## Skipped (video-only)\r\n- S#·Q# (set item <n>) — reason\r\n\r\n**>>> NEXT <<<** S# · Q# (set item <n>)\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe Done line\'s percentages double as an integrity record: if a later re-read disagrees, the transcription of that question is suspect.\r\n\r\n## RC / other sections\r\n\r\nIf Joshua only wants LR, skip Reading Comp. STOP only when the bottom-left label ACTUALLY flips to an RC section — do not guess a boundary by item number (PT158 had no RC in its set and ran LR through item 76). When it does flip, tell Joshua that test\'s LR is done, update `LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md`, and name the next test. If doing RC, ask once how to handle the shared passage.\r\n\r\n## Definition of done\r\n\r\n- Every authored-explanation question in every requested section is in the single `PT<NNN>-explanations.md`, verbatim, with the run\'s verified style mode represented accurately.\r\n- Every captured block passes the "5 choices + tags + percentages + exactly one Correct" check.\r\n- The output file\'s `## S# · Q#` headings reconcile with the set-log structure plus the progress log\'s Done / Skipped entries. No section may be missing behind a misleading `COMPLETE` marker.\r\n- Video-only skips and any type-inferred / unreadable items are listed in the progress log.\r\n- `LR-TRANSCRIPTION-PROJECT.md` updated and the next test named.\r\n- **Close the loop in the set log:** if the PT has an entry in `output/lsat-lab-set-log.md`, append one line to that PT\'s block: `- **Transcribed:** <YYYY-MM-DD> -> Explanations/PT<NNN>-explanations.md (<done> done, <skipped> skipped)` and refresh the file\'s `updated:` frontmatter. The shared ledger then shows both halves of a PT\'s lifecycle: built AND transcribed.\r\n- Report a short final summary: questions done, skips, flags.\r\n\r\n## Do NOT\r\n- Do not capture only one style when a style selector EXISTS — capture all three (Standard, Brief, Another Way). The only time one block is correct is a genuine single-style question with no selector (verify, then use `### Written (single style)` + the callout note). Never fabricate three styles to fill the template.\r\n- Do not make per-question files (one file per test, always).\r\n- Do not paraphrase, "improve", or fabricate percentages/tags/explanations.\r\n- Do not click "Request Written" (generates non-authored content).\r\n- Do not open a new tab or re-navigate when Joshua already has the set open; reuse his tab, matched by URL.\r\n- Do not use em dashes in your own prose (the answer-choice delimiter, and any real em dash inside a verbatim stimulus, are the only exceptions).\r\n- Do not stop for routine questions mid-run; pause only for genuine blockers (missing data, extension disconnect, RC-format decision, section-coverage confirmation).\r\n\r\n'}]
