[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '#!/usr/bin/env python3\n"""Inventory the Gemini session transcripts and map them to student folders.\n\nRead-only. Writes nothing, moves nothing — it prints a report that the `ingest`\nskill (and a human) act on. It exists because the mapping step is the expensive\npart: transcript filenames are inconsistent ("Dakota", "sofia", "LSAT Session\nbetween Joshua and Meghan Mullins", "Meeting started <date>"), and every past\nsession re-derived that mapping by hand.\n\nFilename shapes actually present in raw/data-about-me/transcripts/:\n    <Name> - <YYYY_MM_DD HH_MM TZ> - Notes by Gemini.docx\n    <Kind> between Joshua and <Name> - <date> - Notes by Gemini.docx\n    Meeting started <date> - Notes by Gemini.docx        <- no name, unresolvable\n\nUsage:\n    python3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py            # summary\n    python3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --unmatched  # only the gaps\n    python3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --json      # machine-readable\n    python3 system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py --text <file.docx>  # dump one transcript as text\n\n--text exists because neither python-docx nor pandoc is installed on the\nWindows box; a .docx is a zip with word/document.xml inside, and that is all a\nreader needs.\n"""\nimport json\nimport os\nimport re\nimport sys\nimport unicodedata\nimport zipfile\n\n# The vault root is two levels up from this file (system/scripts/), so the\n# script works on any machine. AI_OS_ROOT overrides it if the layout ever moves.\nBRAIN = os.environ.get(\n    "AI_OS_ROOT",\n    os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))),\n)\nTRANSCRIPTS = f"{BRAIN}/raw/data-about-me/transcripts"\nSTUDENTS = f"{BRAIN}/students"\n\n# The date stamp is the one reliable anchor. Everything after it is an artifact\n# label that varies ("Notes by Gemini", "Chat", "Recording-captions"), so match\n# the stamp itself and treat the remainder as the artifact kind.\nDATE_RE = re.compile(r"\\s*-?\\s*(\\d{4})_(\\d{2})_(\\d{2})\\s+(\\d{2})_(\\d{2})\\s+\\w+\\s*-?\\s*", re.I)\nARTIFACT_RE = re.compile(r"^(Notes by Gemini|Chat|Recording-captions|Transcript)$", re.I)\nBETWEEN_RE = re.compile(r"^(?P<kind>.*?)\\bbetween Joshua and\\b\\s*(?P<name>.+)$", re.I)\nMEETING_RE = re.compile(r"^Meeting started\\b", re.I)\n\n\ndef slug(s):\n    s = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", s).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode()\n    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "-", s.lower()).strip("-")\n\n\nFORMER = os.path.join(STUDENTS, "non-students")\n\n\ndef student_dirs():\n    """Current student folders plus former ones under students/non-students/.\n\n    Former students moved there on 2026-09-10 and are excluded from every\n    sweep, but a transcript of theirs still needs to *resolve* -- otherwise it\n    surfaces as "no student folder" every run and tempts a session to create\n    a duplicate folder for someone who already has a record.\n    """\n    out = {}\n    for base in (STUDENTS, FORMER):\n        if not os.path.isdir(base):\n            continue\n        for n in sorted(os.listdir(base)):\n            p = os.path.join(base, n)\n            if os.path.isdir(p) and not n.startswith("_") and n != "non-students":\n                out[n] = p\n    return out\n\n\ndef is_former(slug_, dirs):\n    return slug_ in dirs and dirs[slug_].startswith(FORMER)\n\n\ndef parse(fname):\n    """-> dict(subject, kind, artifact, date, time, raw)"""\n    stem = os.path.splitext(fname)[0]\n    m = DATE_RE.search(stem)\n    date = f"{m.group(1)}-{m.group(2)}-{m.group(3)}" if m else None\n    time = f"{m.group(4)}:{m.group(5)}" if m else None\n    if m:\n        head = stem[:m.start()].strip()\n        tail = stem[m.end():].strip()\n    else:\n        head, tail = stem.strip(), ""\n    artifact = tail if ARTIFACT_RE.match(tail) else (tail or "unknown")\n    head = re.sub(r"\\s*-\\s*$", "", head).strip()\n\n    if MEETING_RE.match(head) or not head:\n        return dict(subject=None, kind="meeting", artifact=artifact,\n                    date=date, time=time, raw=head or stem)\n    b = BETWEEN_RE.match(head)\n    if b:\n        kind = re.sub(r"\\s+", " ", b.group("kind")).strip() or "session"\n        return dict(subject=re.sub(r"\\s+", " ", b.group("name")).strip(),\n                    kind=kind.lower(), artifact=artifact, date=date, time=time, raw=head)\n    return dict(subject=re.sub(r"\\s+", " ", head).strip(), kind="session",\n                artifact=artifact, date=date, time=time, raw=head)\n\n\ndef load_aliases():\n    """Parse students/_aliases.md -> {alias_slug: folder_slug}.\n\n    Only the \'## Aliases\' section is a mapping; the other sections are human\n    notes about names deliberately without folders.\n    """\n    path = os.path.join(STUDENTS, "_aliases.md")\n    out = {}\n    if not os.path.isfile(path):\n        return out\n    section = None\n    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:\n        for line in f:\n            if line.startswith("## "):\n                section = line.strip().lower()\n                continue\n            if section == "## aliases":\n                m = re.match(r"\\s*-\\s*(.+?)\\s*->\\s*([a-z0-9-]+)\\s*$", line)\n                if m:\n                    out[slug(m.group(1))] = m.group(2)\n            elif section and section.startswith("## prospects"):\n                # Consulted but never taught, so no student record. Still prospects\n                # on the same path as everyone else -- see CONTEXT.md. Mapped to\n                # None so they stop surfacing as unresolved every run.\n                m = re.match(r"\\s*-\\s*([^—-]+?)\\s*(?:—.*)?$", line)\n                if m:\n                    out[slug(m.group(1))] = None\n    return out\n\n\ndef match_student(subject, dirs, aliases=None):\n    """Map a transcript subject to a student folder. Returns (slug, how) or (None, why).\n\n    Deliberately conservative: exact slug, then first-name, then containment.\n    An ambiguous first name (two students named Sofia) returns None so a human\n    decides rather than the script silently picking one.\n    """\n    if not subject:\n        return None, "no subject in filename"\n    s = slug(subject)\n    if aliases and s in aliases:\n        target = aliases[s]\n        if target is None:\n            return None, "prospect, no record"\n        return (target, "alias") if target in dirs else (None, f"alias -> missing folder \'{target}\'")\n    if s in dirs:\n        return s, "exact"\n    first = s.split("-")[0]\n    hits = [d for d in dirs if d == first or d.startswith(first + "-")]\n    if len(hits) == 1:\n        return hits[0], "first-name"\n    if len(hits) > 1:\n        return None, f"ambiguous: {\', \'.join(hits)}"\n    hits = [d for d in dirs if first and (first in d.split("-"))]\n    if len(hits) == 1:\n        return hits[0], "contains"\n    if len(hits) > 1:\n        return None, f"ambiguous: {\', \'.join(hits)}"\n    return None, "no student folder"\n\n\ndef docx_text(path):\n    """Paragraph text of a .docx, one paragraph per line, no dependencies."""\n    with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as z:\n        xml = z.read("word/document.xml").decode("utf-8", "replace")\n    paras = re.split(r"</w:p>", xml)\n    out = []\n    for para in paras:\n        text = "".join(re.findall(r"<w:t[^>]*>([^<]*)</w:t>", para))\n        if text.strip():\n            out.append(text)\n    return "\\n".join(out)\n\n\ndef docx_words(path):\n    """Rough word count from a .docx without any third-party dependency."""\n    try:\n        with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as z:\n            xml = z.read("word/document.xml").decode("utf-8", "replace")\n    except Exception:\n        return None\n    text = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", " ", xml)\n    return len(text.split())\n\n\ndef main():\n    args = set(sys.argv[1:])\n    if "--text" in sys.argv:\n        target = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--text") + 1]\n        if not os.path.isabs(target) and not os.path.isfile(target):\n            target = os.path.join(TRANSCRIPTS, target)\n        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")\n        print(docx_text(target))\n        return 0\n    if not os.path.isdir(TRANSCRIPTS):\n        print(f"no transcript dir: {TRANSCRIPTS}", file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n    dirs = student_dirs()\n    aliases = load_aliases()\n    rows = []\n    for f in sorted(os.listdir(TRANSCRIPTS)):\n        if f.startswith(".") or not f.lower().endswith((".docx", ".txt")):\n            continue\n        p = os.path.join(TRANSCRIPTS, f)\n        info = parse(f)\n        sl, how = match_student(info["subject"], dirs, aliases)\n        rows.append(dict(file=f, **info, student=sl, match=how,\n                         former=bool(sl and is_former(sl, dirs)),\n                         words=docx_words(p) if f.lower().endswith(".docx") else None,\n                         bytes=os.path.getsize(p)))\n\n    if "--json" in args:\n        print(json.dumps(rows, indent=2))\n        return 0\n\n    matched = [r for r in rows if r["student"] and not is_former(r["student"], dirs)]\n    former = [r for r in rows if r["student"] and is_former(r["student"], dirs)]\n    declared = [r for r in rows if not r["student"] and r["match"] == "prospect, no record"]\n    unmatched = [r for r in rows if not r["student"] and r["match"] != "prospect, no record"]\n\n    if "--unmatched" not in args:\n        print(f"transcripts: {len(rows)} files, {len(matched)} mapped, "\n              f"{len(former)} former students, {len(declared)} prospects (no record), "\n              f"{len(unmatched)} unmapped, {len(dirs)} student folders\\n")\n        by = {}\n        for r in matched:\n            by.setdefault(r["student"], []).append(r)\n        print("MAPPED")\n        for s in sorted(by):\n            ds = sorted(x["date"] or "?" for x in by[s])\n            print(f"  {s:20s} {len(by[s]):2d}  {ds[0]} .. {ds[-1]}")\n        print()\n        if former:\n            byf = {}\n            for r in former:\n                byf.setdefault(r["student"], []).append(r)\n            print("FORMER (students/non-students/ -- record exists, excluded from sweeps)")\n            for s in sorted(byf):\n                ds = sorted(x["date"] or "?" for x in byf[s])\n                print(f"  {s:20s} {len(byf[s]):2d}  {ds[0]} .. {ds[-1]}")\n            print()\n\n    print("UNMAPPED (needs a decision — new student folder, alias, or skip)")\n    for r in sorted(unmatched, key=lambda x: (x["match"], x["subject"] or "")):\n        subj = r["subject"] or "(none)"\n        print(f"  {subj:28s} {r[\'kind\']:14s} {r[\'date\'] or \'?\':10s}  {r[\'match\']}")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == "__main__":\n    sys.exit(main())\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\ntitle: Student name aliases\ntype: operating-layer\ntags: [students, operating-layer]\ncreated: 2026-07-20\nupdated: 2026-09-08\nstatus: active\nsubject: Student name aliases\naliases: []\n---\n\n# Student name aliases\n\nMaps the names that show up in transcript filenames, calendar invites, and email\nto the canonical student folder slug in `students/`.\n\nIt exists because Gemini names its exports from the meeting title, which is\nwhatever was typed at booking time: `Sofia`, `sofia`, `Sofia Minassian`,\n`LSAT Session between Joshua and Meghan Mullins` all mean one student. Without\nthis file every pass re-derives the same mapping by hand and gets it slightly\ndifferently.\n\nRead by `system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py`. Format is one bullet per\nalias, `alias -> folder-slug`. Matching is case- and punctuation-insensitive,\nso only add entries the automatic matcher cannot get on its own (exact slug,\nfirst name, and unambiguous containment are already handled).\n\n## Aliases\n\n- Adriana -> adriana-varea\n- Adriana Varea -> adriana-varea\n- Isabella Lorenzo -> bella-lorenzo\n- Kaden Forges -> kaden-forges\n- Thaniuska Vivas -> thaniuska-vivas\n- Yahunathan -> yahunatan-zetvy\n- YahuNathan -> yahunatan-zetvy\n- Yahunatan -> yahunatan-zetvy\n- Ore -> ore\n- Oreoluwa -> ore\n- Oreoluwa James -> ore\n- Yahunatan Zetvy -> yahunatan-zetvy\n- Asia -> asia\n- Celia -> celia-cutler\n- Celia Cutler -> celia-cutler\n- Charles -> charles-moore\n- Charles Moore -> charles-moore\n- Fernande -> fernande-charleus\n- Fernande charleus -> fernande-charleus\n- Steven -> steven-morris\n- Steven Morris -> steven-morris\n- Vinay -> vinay-kalva\n- Vinay Kalva -> vinay-kalva\n- Whit -> whit-wilson\n- Whit Wilson -> whit-wilson\n\n## Prospects, no record yet\n\nConsulted but never taught, so they have not earned a student record (see\n`CONTEXT.md`: a taught session is what earns a folder). They are **still\nprospects on the same path as everyone else** and remain worth following up.\nThis section previously read "Not students", which was wrong: Yahunatan was a\nconsultation on 2026-07-07 and a paying student by 2026-07-15.\n\nListed here so they stop surfacing as unresolved on every inventory run, and so\nfollow-up stays possible. ## Tool: exec

const listing = await tools.exec_command({cmd:"gws calendar calendarList list --format json","shell":"powershell","workdir":"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os","yield_time_ms":30000,"max_output_tokens":15000});
const raw = listing.output.slice(listing.output.indexOf("{"));
const calendars = JSON.parse(raw).items.filter(c => c.id && c.summary !== "Holidays in United States");
const start = "2026-08-01T00:00:00-04:00";
const end = "2026-09-24T16:41:00-04:00";
const results = await Promise.all(calendars.map(async c => {
  const params = JSON.stringify({calendarId:c.id,timeMin:start,timeMax:end,singleEvents:true,orderBy:"startTime",maxResults:250});
  const ps = `$params = '${params.replaceAll("'","''")}'; $d = gws calendar events list --params $params | ConvertFrom-Json; $d.items | ForEach-Object { [pscustomobject]@{cal='${c.summary.replaceAll("'","''")}'; id=$_.id; date=$_.start.dateTime; summary=$_.summary; description=$_.description; attendees=(($_.attendees | ForEach-Object { $_.email }) -join ','); notes=(($_.attachments | ForEach-Object { $_.fileId }) -join ',')} } | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 3 -Compress`;
  const r = await tools.exec_command({cmd:ps,shell:"powershell",workdir:"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os",yield_time_ms:30000,max_output_tokens:25000});
  return {cal:c.summary,output:r.output};
}));
const events = [];
for (const r of results) {
  try {
    const ix = r.output.indexOf("[");
    if (ix >= 0) {
      const jsonText = r.output.slice(ix).trim();
      const parsed = JSON.parse(jsonText);
      events.push(...parsed);
    } else if (r.output.trim().startsWith("{")) {
      events.push(JSON.parse(r.output.trim()));
    }
  } catch { text(`PARSE_FAIL ${r.cal}: ${r.output.slice(0,1000)}`); }
}
store("events_all_aug_sep", events);
const uniq = new Map();
for (const e of events) {
  const key = `${e.date}|${e.summary}|${e.attendees}`;
  if (!uniq.has(key)) uniq.set(key,e);
}
const selected = [...uniq.values()].filter(e => /session|consultation|lesson/i.test(`${e.summary} ${e.description||""}`));
text(JSON.stringify({calendarCount:calendars.length,events:events.length,unique:uniq.size,selected:selected.map(e=>({cal:e.cal,date:e.date,summary:e.summary,attendees:e.attendees,notes:!!e.notes,description:(e.description||"").slice(0,100)}))}));

 .map(e=>`${e.date} | ${e.summary} | ${e.cal} | ${e.attendees} | note=${!!e.notes}`)
 .join("\n");
text(xs);

Joshua Fisseha \r\ninstructed Celia Syrocki that they could flag questions directly on the Law School Admission Test site so the system \r\nwould save them for future review if they could not find a specific item during the session.\r\ndownload.bin:43:* Two Approaches to Parallel Questions: Joshua Fisseha outlined the two primary methods for \r\napproaching parallel questions: conditional logic diagramming and a conversational approach. Joshua Fisseha explained \r\nthat conditional logic utilizes repetitive terms and conditional indicators to create a template, whereas the \r\nconversational approach involves evaluating the argument by accepting the evidence and trying to argue the opposite \r\nconclusion through an objection or by calling out an assumption.\r\ndownload.bin:46:* The Conversational Approach and Theory-Neutral Facts: Joshua Fisseha used an example regarding not \r\ngetting a spouse a birthday gift to illustrate the conversational approach, where Celia Syrocki provided an objection \r\nstating that gifts were bought during other holidays. Joshua Fisseha emphasized that people naturally view facts \r\nthrough a biased, interpretive lens, but analytical reasoning requires treating facts as theory-neutral to effectively \r\nidentify hidden assumptions or construct valid objections.\r\ndownload.bin:49:* Emotional Engagement for Information Retention: Joshua Fisseha advised Celia Syrocki to react \r\nemotionally to every statement they read when practicing, explaining that emotional engagement significantly improves \r\nmemory retention compared to reviewing a flat list of facts. Following this discussion, Joshua Fisseha presented a \r\nchallenging Law School Admission Test practice question for Celia Syrocki to solve.\r\ndownload.bin:50:* Analysis of the Traffic Fatality Practice Question: Celia Syrocki selected answer choice D for the \r\npractice question, which stated that drivers aged 19 and younger make up only 7% of registered drivers but are \r\nresponsible for over 14% of traffic fatalities. Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki analyzed the argument, categorizing \r\nthe first statement as a normative claim and the statistical statement as a correlation, noting that the argument \r\nincorrectly assumes a causal relationship between demographic percentages and basic driving skills.\r\ndownload.bin:51:* Transition to Conditional Parallel Question: Following the correlation-based question, Joshua \r\nFisseha transitioned the session to a level 5 conditional parallel question. Celia Syrocki selected answer choice D \r\nand explained that they used conditional logic diagramming to solve it, noting that words like "no" frequently \r\nindicate conditional statements on the Law School Admission Test that describe relationships between concepts, such as \r\ncentrally planned economic systems and the efficient allocation of resources.\r\ndownload.bin:52:* Conditional Logic Diagramming and Chaining: Celia Syrocki and Joshua Fisseha discussed diagramming \r\nconditional logic arguments, specifically chaining premises together and applying contrapositives. Celia Syrocki \r\nexplained how they mapped out a centrally planned economy premise requiring a national debt of less than 5% of GDP to \r\nefficiently allocate resources, using the contrapositive to chain the statements together (00:59:06). Joshua Fisseha \r\nnoted that students should identify conditional indicator words and move from the most concrete, sturdy grounds to \r\nsofter grounds when building chains (01:00:42).\r\ndownload.bin:53:* Identifying Necessary Conditions and Indicator Words: Joshua Fisseha and Celia Syrocki examined \r\nindicator words in conditional statements, highlighting that finding explicit terms like "necessary condition" is very \r\nrare (01:02:17). Celia Syrocki identified efficient allocation as the necessary condition for achieving a national \r\ndebt of less than 5% of GDP. Joshua Fisseha prompted Celia Syrocki to analyze the final sentence of the argument as a \r\ncontrapositive of the chained conditional premises (01:03:01).\r\ndownload.bin:54:* Matching Repeating Concepts in Complex Arguments: Joshua Fisseha guided Celia Syrocki through \r\nrecognizing overlapping and repeating concepts across premises, such as centrally planned economic systems, efficient \r\nresource allocation, and national debt percentages relative to GDP (01:05:49). Joshua Fisseha explained that once \r\nrepeating concepts are identified and labeled with letter templates, polarity (yeses and nos) can be stripped away to \r\nfocus purely on the direct chain or contrapositive structure of the argument (01:09:32) (01:11:50).\r\ndownload.bin:55:* Elimination Strategies for Parallel Reasoning Questions: Celia Syrocki asked whether words not \r\npresent in the original argument, such as "no" in answer choice E, could be used to immediately eliminate options \r\nwithout working through the entire conditional logic (01:25:24). Joshua Fisseha confirmed that broadly speaking, this \r\nelimination strategy can work based on structural words, but warned that test creators can use sneaky, interchangeable \r\nphrases that require careful attention (01:27:31).\r\ndownload.bin:56:* Future Study Direction and Upcoming Drills: Joshua Fisseha discussed the possibility of introducing \r\nintensive "recall and rephrase" drills or full practice sections to rapidly improve scores, though they noted these \r\nexercises are extremely demanding (01:31:14).
